" Whether the innagement of Ena Fire Project Ena 

Colliery under Area VII (Kustore ) of M ) , B . C . C . L .

6 . The learned counsel for thic workcian conceded that 
ilie lomestic enquily was fair and proper and accordingly 
The Icarned counsel of both the sides have been heard on 
merit, How in the circumstances, we have to see whether 
here was evidence beforç thc Enquiry Officer to hold the 
concerned workinan guilty of misconduct. 


2 , As reported tbe most unpleasant occurance took place 
on 27- 2 - 86 at about 10 .00 A . M . with Dr. R . Ojha, Senior 
Medical Oncer , East Bhuggataih Colliery dispensary wbile 
he was on official duty in his chamber . The concerned 
workman Shri Pramod Kumar Singh met the dector in bis 
chamber and told that his mother way ailing seriously . He 
also insisted upon the doctor to go to his residence but the 
doctor advised him to take Ambulance and bring his mother 
to hospital. As alleged there was hot exchange of words 
and the concerned workman was starcd to have become 
very furious and hc hurled Althy abuses upon the doctor 
caught hold of him by the collar of his shirt and wanted 
to assault him with lathi, The concerned workman was 
pushed out of the chamber . At this stage I would like to 
mention that the concerned workman was a Dozer Operator 
of Enu Fire Project in Ena Colliery and his futher Shri 
K . P . Singh was an Overman of East Bhuiggatdih collicry . 
The doctor subnitted report (Ext. M -6 ) to the Agent, East 
Bhuggatdih collicry for nccessary action and also to provide 
him Security for his life . That written report was the 
basis for issuarce of charge hout Ext. M - 1. The chargesheet 
datod 27 -2 - 86 was issued alleging that the concerned work 
man abused the doctor saulted him by catching hold of 
the collar of his shirt and also tried to beat him with Jathi. 
The action of the concerned workman was stated to be a 
serious misconduct in terms of the certified standing orders 
29 (5 ) which reads as "Fighting, rlutous, disorderly and in 
clecent hehaviour." 


7 . At the first instance a legal aspect of the matter was 
canvassed by the learned counsel for the workman . It was 
stated that the occurrence toik place within the jurisdiction 
of East Bhuggatdih colliery whereas the concerned work . 
man is an employee of Ena Colliery . It was urged that 
these are two different collierias and the Agent of Ena 
Colliery had no jurisdiction to dismiss a workman . It was 
contended that the managemçnt cannot be tho custodian of 
the morals of its employees and any act committed beyond 
the premises of the establishment or vicinity thereof cannot 
le trcated as subversive of disciplinary . However , the 
Jearned counsel for the managemeut submitted that there 
may be several colljeries within one and the same aroa , 
It was urged that the terms of reference speaks in very many 
words that the management of Ene Colliery is under Area 
No , VIII Kustore of M / s. B . C . C . L . MW - 1 in his opening 
line of cross- examination stated that Bhusgatdih Colliery 
is under Are No. VIII. It is not that the incident took 
place with different persons in different areas. Since the 
Area was one and the same it was within the competence of 
Fna colliery to take action against tho corcerned workman. 
.3t this stage I any further add that in industrial affairs 
relationship between the employer and the workmen is the 
mutter of paramount importance . Only the smooth rc 
lutionship will allow smooth working of any industry . In 
this case the act done by the concerned workiman was not 
solely confined to the private Ilfo. He himself mishehaved 
rather exhibited an act of gross indiscipline with the doctor 
of Bhuggatdih Colliery when the doctor wns on duty . The 
incident has got direct link with the general relationshir 
of «mployer and employee. In such a situation the act 
committed even outside the establishment or vicinity thereof 
will amount to an act subversive of discipline . In such 
view of the mottar T am constrained to hold that Ena Collierv 
had every jurisdiction to issue chargesheet and proceed 
Agninst the concerned workman . 


3 . Thc concerned workman was called upon to cxplain 
as to why the disciplinary action should not be taken 
against him and he was suspended with effect from 28 - 2 - 86 . 
The concerned workman submitted his cxplanation (Ext. 
M - 3 ) which was found not satisfactory and simulta 

cously here was an order for domestic enquiry , Shri R . N . 
Singh , Personnel Officer , South Tharia Collicry was appoin 
ted an Enquiry Officer (M - 4 ). The Enquiry Officer submitted 
his report (Ext. M - 7 ) which was considered and the con 
cerned workman was dismissed on 12- 7 - 86 (Ext. M - 9 ) . 


4 . The workmen and the managemont have both filed 
their W . S . The concerned workman denied each and 
every allegation levelled against him in the chargesheet and 
he further stated that he had gone to tho doctor of the 
Post Bhuggatdih Colllery dispensary and also narrated the 
serious condition of his mother. He also stated that his 
father was not present at the house and in big absence 
he had to rush to the hospital for treatment of his mother . 
As stated by thc workman he tried to impress upon the 
doctor that his mother was not in a position to be carried 
to the hospital even by Ambulance and therefore requiested 
him to go to the residence but the doctor point blank re 
fascd . Utimately he had to remove his mother to a nurg 
ing home at Jharia . He has denied all other allegations like 
hurling abuses and catching of the collar of the shirt. 


8 . During the course of argument the learned counsel 
for the workman also wanted to bring on the record a 
nhoto conv of the iudgement of the Criminal Court just to 
show that the concerned workman was found not guilty in 
the criminal trial and he wno ncquittert. Definitely the 
cocuntent was not placed hefore the Enquiry Oficer in 
the doniestic prouiry . It was contended on hihaf of the 
management that the Tribunal cannot permit fresh evidence 
in he adduced on mcrit of the case when the domes 
tice enquiry has heen held to be fair and proper , 
Reliance was also placed iron a interament of the Hon ble 
High Court of Patna reported in 1978 LIC at pater 494 M / 9. 
Rohtas tudustries to resem Their workmen , in the afore 
snid nse the Court halil thot hy the introduction of Section 
11A the Industrial Tribunal is now clothed with the power 
to reappraise the evidence in the domestic cnauiry and satis 
fv itself whether said rvidence establishes the misconduct 
alleged against in cmniovec The Tribunal, therefore has 
the power to examine the evidence lent in the domostic on 
ouiry and came to a different conclusior but the material , 
on which it c111 do < will be confinell in the evidence led 
in the domestic enquiry as is clear from the proviso of the 
Section 111 , The Hon ble Court further held that the Tri 
bural is not nermitted to take any fresh evidence in reaching 
such a conclusion ,

submitted that the action of the management in dismiss

By insupling the same unfair labour practice , the manufc 

muat trustcured ine workmen , who was the General

Guicen 10 Borivali by the order cluted 13 - 7 - 1989 , 
to stop hin to altend to the work of the Union 
promptly and with a view to break thc Union , 
The inuilstrid dispute was thereatter raused by the 
Union by the letter dated 15 - 7 - 1989 in respect of 
the uansici order of the workman . While that 
industrial de pute was pendion before the Concilia 
tion Officer , the management by their order dated 
3 . 8 - 1989 transteried the workinan from Bombay 
10 Mour Mandi in Punjab , with a mulallde inten 
tion and with a view to break ibc workmen 
Union. While muny technical assistant senior in 
service to the workman , werd working in Bombay 
since their appointments in 1976 and 1978 , etc ., 
the workman, who was junior to themn , came to be 
transferred. That transter order dated 3 - 8 - 1989 
was the 8th transfer of the workman since his 
appointment in service in November 1978 at Hydera 
bad . Eventhough the cuncilliation proceedings were 
pending before the conciliation osticer , in pursuance 
of the transfer order duted 3 - 8 - 1989 the workman 
was relieved from bis duties on 14 - 8 - 1989 to join 
the new place of work . This was also an unfair 
labour practice on the part of the inadugement. 
By issuing the relieving oider , the management 
contravened tbe provisions of sub sections 1 and 3 
of Section 33 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 , 
as tho service conditions applicable to the workman 
were changed with etlect from 14 - 8 - 1989. The 
transfer order to Mour Manji was issued by the 
management because of the trade union activities 
carried out by the workman . Thut relicving order 
dated 14- 8 - 1989 was not accepted by the work 
man , as it was a malalide one . The management 
further issued a third transfer order dated 23 - 9 - 1989 
transfering him to Hissar in Haryurs . This trans 
fcr order was also isslied with a view to victimise 

the workman because of his trikde union activities . 
The workman wily relieved without providing any substi 

tute in his place at Bombay. There are at present 
three vacancies of technical workmen at Cotton 
Green where the workman was working. The work 
man did not accept the transfer order to Mour Mandi 
and he continued presenting himseli for work at 
the place in Bombay since 16 - 8 - 1989 and onwards. 
However, no work used to be signed to him , 
and he was prevented from doing any work . He

however , against S . K . Lekhi, Contractor of the said man 
agement The workman has only claimed difference of wagos 
from 1st January, 1983 to 31st May, 1988 for a total period

Rs. 8123. Thus the Contractor hy name is neither a party to 
the dispute nor the claim is in accordance with the terms of 
reference . Such a claim , if any, can be inade under Scc . 
33 - C ( 2 ) of the I. D . Act, 1947, 

7. I have gone through the statement of claim and docu 
ments filed by the workman, The workmaq has neither chal 
lenged his termination order nor prayed for reinstatement in 
case termination order is found to be illegal. He has only 
claimed difference of wages for a particular period. Hence 
no relief as claimed can be granted in this case . Award is 
passed accordingly with no order as to costs.

AWARD 
The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour in 
cxercise of the powers conferred on them by clause ( d ) of 
sub -section ( 1 ) and sub -section (2A ) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 has referred the following dis 
pute to this Tribunal for adjudication vide Ministry s Order 
No. L - 22012 ( 186 ) / 88 -DIV . B dated the 27th April, 1989. 

SCHEDULE 
" Whether the action of the Management of Adjai- II 

Unit of Girmit Colliery , Sripur Area of M / 9. Eastern 
Coalfields Ltd ., P . O . Charanrur , Dist, Burdwan in 
terminating the services of Sri Anil Dey. Lamp 
Chargeman on the ground of superannuating w .e . t. 
1 - 7 - 1988, was justified ? If not, to what relief the 
workman concerned is entitled 7 " 


5 . The management has produced the B Form Register ! 
which were rc -constructed after nationalisation Exts . M - 3 
und M - 4 . In these registers tho year of birth of the con 
cerned workman has been recorded as 1928 . The manage 
mont has also produced the service cxcerpts Ext . M - 1 which 
was duly signed by the concerned workman . In this service 
cxcerpts his year of birth has been recorded as 1928. By 
placing and referring those documents the learned lawyer 
for the management has left no stone unturned to convince 
tho Tiibunal that the year of hirth of the concerned work 
man was rightly recorded 19 1928 . He has further urged 
before mc that Sri Anil Dey the concerned workman is a 
literate man and he has put his signature in English on all 
the Jocuments and from that this Tribunal should conclude 
that the workman put his signature knowing fully that his 
year of birth was recorded as 1928 . He has further urged 
hefore me that as the concerned workman did not raise any 
dignute earlier he is not entitled to get any relief in this 
Reference case , 


Be that as it may , the workmon has produced his Identity 
Card Fxt. W - 1. In that Identity Card his year of birth 
has been recorded as 1930 . The leamci Tawver for the 
management has urged before me that this document hag 
hecn manufactured for the purpose of this case . But consi 
dering the nature and condition of the document ( Identity 
Card ) I am unable to look cve to eve with the lenmed Lawyer 
for the management. Sri P . Mahanti MW - 1 has admitted 
thut Sri Anil Dey win an einploure from hefore nationalisa 
tion and there was a B For Rezister brfore nationaligation , 
But the management has failed to produce the B Form 
Register which wig premored before natinnalisation on the 
plea that the gaid register is not traceable. But the fact 
remains that the manurement has failed to produce the 
earliest docunicnt regarding the are of the workman . We 
find from the statement of the concerned workman that he 
read only unto Class IV . In my opinion it will be against 
prudence to hold that he understood cverything of the 
document hefore putting his signature . 


6 . Weighing the evidence on record and considering the 
facts and circumstances of the instant case , I find that in a 
case like the present one. The workman must be given an 
opportunity to ret his age assesseit ihrnurh Ame Assessment 
Medical Board as there are two sels of ( ocuments showing 
his reas of hirth both as 1931) And 1928 But I must say 
that the workman did not raise the dispute at the earliest 
opportunity . So he must be penulised in some form . 


2 . The case of the union in bric is that Sri Anil Dey 
the concerned workman was Lamp Chargeman of Adjoy - II 
Unit of Girmint Colliery , Sripur Area under M / s. Eastern 
Coalfields Ltd . He joined in the service in 1945 before na 
tionalisation and as per Mines Rules his service particulars 
including his year of birth were recorded in tho statutory 
register known as B Form Register . In the said B Form 
Register and also in the Form Al of the Provident Fund 
Account his year of birth had been recorded as 1930 . After 
nationalisation he was given an Identity Card showing his 
year of birth as 1930 , But subsequently he came to know 
that in the reconstructed B Form Register his year of birth 
has been recorded as 1928 . He immediately lodged a comi 
plaint on 31 - 1 - 1988 and reauested the management to re 
tain his year of birth as 1930 or alternatively to get his age 
2968 GI/ 91 – 3 


7 . In the result I find that the termination of service of 
Sri Anil Dev the concerned workman on the ground of 
suderannuation w . e. f. 1 - 7 -88 we not justifiet. The manage 
ment shall refer the concerned workman to Anex Medical 
Board for determination of his age . 
( A ) If the Aner Medical Board finds that the workman 

attained the age of 60 years in 1988 , then the 

workman shall not get any relief in this case , 
( b ) But if the Apex Medical Board finds that Sri Anil 

Dey attained the age of 60 years after 1 - 7 -88 , 
then the workman shall get 50 % of the wages for 
the period from 1 - 7 - 88 till the date of attaining

give him notice or pay him 
compensation. 


notice pay besides retrenchment 


4 . In fact there had been no termination of his services 
as alleged by the workman , Regular recruitment in the banks 
is made as per Government Policy and after satisfying other 
eligibility criteria . The workman s name was never spon

7 . In para 1 of his atidavit the workman has deposed 
that during the period October 1979 to 1982, he had worked 
for 79 duys. In para 2 of his cross examination , he has 
deposed that for these 79 days hc hall worked as a tempar 
ary workman . In the writtel arguments filed on behalt 
of the workman by Shri V . K . Gurta , it is stated that 
the workman was appointed in leave vacancies during 1979 
to 1982 . 

8 . The above facts thus go to show that his appointrucnt 
was temporary and that lou wat in leuve vacancies and 
not as permanent employce against, permnent vacancy . 
Hence , I find that the case set up by the workman in the 
claim statement that he was appointed as a peon and changed 
in the rcjoinder by alleging that he was appointed as mog 
senger cum water boy against a permanent vacancy is not 
correct. 


9. The dispute between the parties is with regard to the 
subsequent period upto 15- 3 - 88. The management s case 18 
that he was engaged as # water boy for filling drinking 
water at the rate of Rs. 10 per fortnight which was subse 
quently raised to Rs. 20 pcr fortnight. On the other 
hand as already remarked he has nowhere alleged in the 
claim statement that he had even worked as a water boy . 
It was however , for the first time in his rejoinder that he 
came out with the case that he had worked as messenger 
cum watcrboy for more than 10 years. In para ( 3 ) of his 
affidavit he has referred to the nature of dutics performed 
by him . The duties described by him are distribution of 
DAK taking bank s books from one table to another and 
filling water and serving water. 


entitled ? 


2 . The case of the workman in short is that he was 
appointed against a permanent vacancy at Mainpuri Branch 
of the bank in October, 1979. Despite his posting as peon 
in the seid branch he was not paid salary of the peon as 
per bipartite settlement. His services were discrutinuert 
by the management w .e .f . 16 - 3 - 78 (should be 16 - 3 - 88 ) 
despite his excellent work and conduct without notice or 
notice pay and payment of retrenchment compensation . The 
action of the management in terminating his acrvices i ; 
illegal and as such họ is entitled to his reinstatement with 
full back wages . 


10 . During his cross examinatiou the workman was 
confronted with two voucher the photostat copies of which 
were filed under the orders of the Tribunal by the manage 
ment on the very date of his cross examination i.e . on 
25- 1 -91. These vouchers are dated 3 - 10 -83 ond 16 - 2 -87. 
Thc workınan has admitted his signatures on them . The 
voucher dated 16 - 2 - 87 shows that he was paid Rs. 20 for 
filling water for the period 1 -2 - 87 to 15 - 2 -87, the second 
vouche r is dated 9 - 10 - 83 . It shows that he was paid Rs. 10 
for water filling. These two vouchers coupled with the 
statement of the workman corroborates the management s 
case set up in nara 2 of the written statement that after 1982 
for Glling drinking waer he was paid at the rate of Rs. 10 
per fortnight in the beginning and then at the rate of Rs. 20 
The fortnight.

getting wages for filling drinking water separately from 
the wages which he was getting for the service rendered by 
him AS messenger. At the vory outset I may state that 
this is not the case of Shri Naresh Chandra . No attempt 
has been made from his side to summon the bank s record 
to prove that he was getting some wages every month from 
the bank for the services rendered by him as messenger.

After 1982, ho simply worked as a Waterboy for filling 
drinking water in the Mainpuri Branch in the years 1984 
to 1987 , in the beginning at the rate of Rs, 10 per fort - 
night and subsequently at the rule of Rs, 20 per fortnight. 
During the period 1984 to ! 987 there did not exist the 
rclation of employer -employee hetween the parties. In

AWARD 
This reference arising out of the Central Government 
Notification No. L - 12011 / 40 / 89 Jated the 19th September, 
relates to the dispute indicated in the Schedule below : 


" Whether the action of tho management of Union Bank 

of India in not allowing Shri Babul Das to officiate 
as Asstt. Head Cashier for the seriod 2 - 2 - 88 to 
25 -2 -88 is justificd ? If not, to what relief is the 
workman entitled ? " 


On receipt of the Notification the Reference was registered 
and notices welc issued to thy parties to file their written 
statements . Timely parties filed their written statement. In 
course of hearing management examined one witness and 
the workinan examined himself only . Aliother witness was 
also examined as court witness, 


Management witness Sri Sukia agair çdmitted in his cross 
examination that he does not renzember whether he allowed 
P , Chakraborty to officiate as Asstt. Head Casheir after 
obtain verbal option . From his evidence it is clear that 
acting Manager did not call for any option in writing either 
from Babul Das Or P . Chakraborty on 2 - 2 - 88 . 

Regarding verlian Gotto witne : : Sukla atid not throw antv 
light in his cviilence . Wirkunan in his rvicence Staicu tut 
acting Branch Manapy dit tro1 call for any option verbally 
from him on 2 - 2 - 88 . This witness further stated that he is 
tho senior most clerk of the said Bank . As per promotion 
policy it is his right to officiate in such leave vacanclos. 
Manuccinont in their written statement para 17 admitted that 
it is the duty of the inanagement to obtain willingness to 
officiate in writing from among the clerical staff to their 
respective Branch Manager . When any occasion for such 
temporary posts occurs management will not as per willing 
nesi on simple senioity busis . Promotion policy of the 
Bank is clear on this point i. e, who 19 Wigiblc to officiate ao 
Asstt . Head Cashier during leave vacancies . 


It is the duty of the Branch Manald: ww obtain in writing 
from cleshs and allowed senior most to officiate in legut 
vacancies as and when necessary Ucs. In the instant case 
I find management did not obtain any option in writing 
from the senior most clork of the Bark and also from P . 
Chakraborty who officiate as Asstt. Tlend Cashior during the 
period from 2 - 2 - 88 to 25- 2 -88. 


Management case inter - alia is that their workman Sri 
Babul Das did not give any option to work as Hend Cashçir 
in the leave vacancy as per promotion policy circulated vide 
staff circular 2806 dated 14 - 5 -85. The then acting Branch 
Manager allowed to P . Chakraborty to officiute in the said 
Icave vacancy that is from 2 - 2 - 88 10 25 - 2 - 88. Sri P . Chakra 
horty served as Asstt. Head Cashier during this period and 
accordingly officiating allowanco was paid to him by manage 
ment. Babul Das has no right to claim any special allowance 
as Asstt. Head Cashier for this period as tic did not work as 
Asstt. Head Cashier for the period from . - 2 - 88 to 25 - 2 - 88 . 
Case of the workman is that Si Bubul Das is senior most 
Asstt.-cum -clerk in the Union Bank of India , Fancy Bazur 
Brunch . Onc Sri Sosodhar Das who served as Asstt. Head 
Canbier of the Bank went on leave from 2 - 2 - 88 to 25- 2 - 88. 
As per promotion policy workman Babul Das heing the senior 
most assistant was to be allowed to offcinta ag Asstt. Hoad 
Casbier for the period from 2 - 2 -88 to 25 - 2 - 88 after obtuining 
his option for that purpose . The then acting Branch Manager 
did not act as per promotion policy . He dlu not obtain any 
option in writing verbally from the senior most clerk of 
the Bank , Acting Branch Manager allowed illegally P . 
Chakraborty to oficinte as Asstt. Head Cashier during the 
above noted poriod. On his protest the then Branch Manager 
agreed to pay the allowances of Assti, Hond Cashier is 
admissible for that period to Babul Das . But on 3 - 3 -89 
Babu Das came to know that officiating allowance was paid 
to P . Chakraborty and thereby he was deprived of his 
legitimate case. 

In support of their respective cases management examincu 
one witness and exhibited some documents. Workman Babul 
Das erumined himself and exhibited some documents , 

Witness Jogonath Clinkrxhorty was evarnined as Court 
witness after hearing both sides. 

Learned Counsel for the mapagement, orgued that Babul 
Das did not opt for officiating as Assit. Head Cashier for 
2968 GI/ 91 - 5 


Therefore I find management lid not follow the procc 
dure as laid down in promotion policy . Witness Sukla 
admitted in his evidence that he does not remember whe 
ther he obtained any verbal option eithes from B . Dag or 
P . Chakratorty . Rahul Day in his cvidence stated that then 
acting Branch Manager dic not call for any verbal option 
and allowed P . Chakraborty to offic are a Asstt. Head 
Cashier under pressure from members of a different union 
of the Bank 


Counsel for management admitted about existence of two 
different union in the bank . I find wakman by cogent 
evidence established that then acting Branch Manager did 
not call for any verhal option from Banui Dus who is the 
senior most clerk of the Branch . Further from cvidence 
on record I lind then acting Branen Manager allowed P . 
Chakraborty to officiale under pressure from members of the 
other union . 


Hence I find management was not justified in not 
allowing Babul Das to officiate an Asstt. Head Cashier for 
the poriod from 2 - 2 - 88 to 25 - 2 - 88 in their Fancy Bazar 
Branch .

11 . This is further to Part I of the Arvard inade 
by my learned predecessor Justice Jamudar on 30th 
June , 1989 . He has held that the Mahanagar Telu 
phone Nigam Limited ( hereafter the Nigam ) is a 
necessary party and will be bound by the final Award 
(para 9 of the Award ) . In para 10 he has held 
that the services of the 7 Workmen werr terminated 
by way of disciplinary action for misconduct and 
that the termination order was not sustainable be 
cause it was made without holding any enquiry . He 
has further ruled that the Society ( Bombay Tele 
phone Co -operative Canteen Society Limited ) and 
the Nigam would be entitled to lead cridence before 
the Tribunal to substantiate the charges levelled 
against the 7 workmen . The society and the work 
men have since adduced evidence before me on the 
charges of misconduct as well as the issue of back 
wages. As Justice Jamdar has already stated the 
respective cases of the parties in Part I of the Award , 
I will not repeat the same again . The issue that 
now arise for adjudication are stated below with my 
findings thereon .

REASONS FOR FINIDINGS 


13 . Issue No. 1 : The Society relies on the deci 
sion of the Supremo Court reportod in 1957 I ILI 
p . 27 Central Provinces Transport Ltd . Vs. Ragh : 1. 
nath Patwardhan for the submission that the present 
dispute is not an industrial dispute within the mean 
ing of section 2 ( k ) in the Industrial Disputes Act 
hereafter the Central Act ) , for the reason that it 
relates to individual Worknico only and a substantial 
number of Workmen have not espoused their carise . 
I am afraid the decision does not help the Manage 
ment at all . In the first place, the matter before the 
Supreme Court arose under the Central Provinces 
and Berar Industrial Disputes Act, which contained 
an express provision in Section 16 that a dispute re 
lating to an individual employee could also be cntci 
tained by Industrial Tribunal. Then after the insur 
tion of section 2 - A in the Central Act in 1965 , the 
Management cannot simply raise the submission . 
Without dialating further , I overrule their objection . 


14 , Issue No. 2 : The objection raised in this 
Issue is also without any substance. No provision 
of law is cited before me, which obligates Workmen 
to exhaust all domcstic reinedies as a condition pre 
cedent to industrial adjudication . This issuç is aky

The Watchman and the Driver states that about 12 
15 Canteen Employees who were squalling on the 
steps Gheroci Virendra Kumar as 11 : cunc down 
from his Chamber , and gave him fist and hick blows. 
The employees also used abusive language The 
Watchinan manage ,l somehow to est Virendra 
Kumar to the door of the Jeep with the help of it Poli 
ce Constable on duty . Manc adds that after getting into 
the Jecp , Virendra Kumar told him to convey to the 
agitating employees that he had not received 
their deinand charter and that he was ready to dis 
cuss their demands on receipt thercot. Manc mana 
ged to pass on this message to the demonstraters 
through the good Otlicus of one Police Srspector. 
The demonstrators allowed the Jeep to go away after 
holding it up for about 20 minutes . Virendra Kumar 
lias substantially deposed to these facts, subject to 
the variation that the incident had occurred on 25th 
Scptember and not on 27111 . Hu has also given the 
names of the Workmen . In rebuttal Jagannath 
Shetty says that they did not go to the Fountain 
Telecon Building at all on 27th or 25th . According 
to him the workers had gone there on 23rd only for 
a peaceful demonstration . 


19 . The Society have adduced evidence only on 
the first and the seventh charge . There is no cvi 
dence whatever on the remaining Charges (that is to 
say charge No . 2 , 3 , 4 , 5 and 6 common for all the 
seven Workmen , and additional Charge No. 8 . 9 and 
10 for Satish Hegde ) . Accordingly I hold that 
these charges remain unproved . I now proccul to 
Charges 1 and 7 . 


20 . Charge No. 1 ; - The Management have not 
led any evidence on this charge . However Work 
man Jagannath Shetty Ex . WW - 2 adınits that the 
seven Workmen left the Canteen premises on 
23- 9 -1982 at 1 P . M . without taking regular permis 
sion of absence from the Manager . According to 
him , they had only informed the Manager on the 
previous day about their intention to close the Cani 
teen on the 23rd in the afternoon . These admissions 
have come in paragraphs 12 and 13 of his cross 
examination . It appears that the Worknen hud on 
that afternoon gone to the Fountain Telephone Ollice 
for staging a peaceful demonstration . Relying on 
Jagannath s admission I hold that this charge stands 
proved against all the 7 Workmen . 


22 . There are a number of serious lacunac in the 
evidence of the Management , in the face of which 
it is not possible to accept their evidence . The first 
major discrepancy is with regard to the date of the 
incident. Thorat and Mane throughout .stick to 27th , 
which also finds place in the charge -sheets while 
Virendra Kumar gives it as 25th . Neither Thorat 
nor Mane knew the names of any of the miscrcants, 
although both of thein affirm that they can identify 
them hy face . Virendra Kumar who has given the 
names of the miscreant Workpien , aclmits in cross 
examination that he did not know their names per 
sonally and came to know them from Thorat, Mane 
and other members of his staff. Thorat and Mano 
falsify him on this aspect , when they concede that 
neither the Police nor any one else ever inquired 
from them the names of the miscreants . Here 1 may 
advert to the fact that Virendra Kumar fad filed a 
complaint about the incident with the Police . But 
that was on 27th , that is, more than 48 hours after 
the incident which , according to him had taken 
place on the afternoon of 25th . Identification parado 
was never arranged by the Police during investigation , 
Nor did the Management show any eagerness to re 
quest the Tribunal to ask the eyewitness to identify 
thc errant Workmen during the course of trial. All 
these factors make it difficult for one to accept the 
evidence of the Management,

cffect that at about 1 P .M . on 27 - 9 - 1982 . Thorat re

24 . In sum , the only charge that stands prorell is 
Charge No. 1, namely that the 7 Workmen had ab 
sented then selvcs from work in the Canteen on the 
afternoon of 23rd September, 1982 without permis 
sion of the Manager. The rest of the charges remain 
uuproved . This disposes of Issue No. 6 . 


25 , Issucs No. 7 and 8 : The only charge which 
stands proved is a very minor onc. Termination of 
Services would obviously be a disproportionately harshi 
punishment for it . The evidence shows that the 
Workmen were not being paid full wages hy the 
Management since long as per directions of the Go 
vernment, with the result that they were compelled 
to take to peaceful demonstrations. This is borne 
out by the evidence of Krishna Murthy WW - 1 , Genc 
ral Secretary of the Hotel L. abour Union ( Red Flag ) , 
Eknath Sawant Ex , WW - 4 , Joint Secretary of the 
aforesaid Union , Workman Jagannath Shytty Ex. 
WW - 2 and Uday Hegde Ex . WW -3 , another Canteen 
enployee. Even on the basis that the absention from 
work on the afternoon of 23rd was for the purpose 
of holding a demonstration in front of the Telcconi 
Building , too serious a view cannot be taken of the 
matter. Ends of justice will be squarely mot, if the 
seven Workmen are deprived of one day s wages 
for absenting themselves from work on 23rd Septem 
ber, 1982 in the afternoon . 


28 . Concerning Workman Jagannath Shetty, the 
Sociсty has adduced the evidence of Chandrakant 
Hawre Ex . SW - 5 , who is Hon . Joint Secretary of the 
Society from 1982. He states that Jagannath Shetty 
had told him two years ago that he was driving a 
Rickshaw in Jogeshwari , The Workman denics to 
have ever made such an admission to Hawre . In 
para 20 of his cross -examination he however admits 
that from September, 1985 he used to earn Rs. 12 
15 per day and that since 1986 he has been on ave 
rage carning Rs. 500 / - per month . He denies the 
Management , ugrastion Diet lis illcrage monthly 
income was Rs. 1200 / - per month . At the instance 
of thic Management this Workman has filed il Xerox 
copy of his Saving Account with the Central Bank 
of India , Prabhaderi Branch ( Ex. W14 ) It shows 
that in April-May , 1986 he had deposited 1 : 8000 / 
out of which he withdrew Rs. 7900 / - in July of the 
same year, leaving a paltry halance of Rs. 155 .40 . 
in the following years there are no deposits at all, 
except small amounts ranging from Rs. 27 - to Rs . 5 , 
by way of interest. In the circumstances it is not 
possible to helieve that Jagannath Shetty had made a 
fortune after terinination of his services . I accept 
his testimony that he was carning Rs. 12 - 15 per day 
between September , 1982 and 1985 and Rs. 500 , 
per month thereafter. In the totality of circumstances , 
grant of 500m of back wages for the cntire period 
shall meet the end of justice . 


26 . The cvidence on thc Workmen s side slows 
that the Management did not allow them to cnter

casc , I hold that all the Workmen would be entitled

titled to full back wages, unless there be some special 
circumstances for any of them . There are such cir

Shetty and Umesh Hegde. I proceed to consider 
them
